   - True Grit was directed by Henry Hathaway.

-- Facts
   [Sports in the New York metropolitan area]
   - New Yorkers can watch the New York Knicks play NBA basketball at Madison Square Garden.
   - The New York Liberty play in the WNBA at Madison Square Garden.
   - The Barclays Center in Brooklyn is home to the Brooklyn Nets NBA basketball team.
   - The Nets began playing in Brooklyn in 2012.
   - The Nets were the first major professional sports team to play in Brooklyn in half a century.
   - The New York Nets were a two-time champion in the American Basketball Association.
   - The New York Nets shared the same home stadium (Nassau Veterans Memorial Coliseum) on Long Island with the NHL's New York Islanders.
   - Julius Erving was a Hall of Fame forward who played for the New York Nets.
   - The American Basketball Association merged with the NBA during the 1976-1977 season.
   - The Nets continued to play on Long Island during the first season of the merger (1976-77).
   - Julius Erving's contract was sold to the Philadelphia 76ers by the time of the 1976-77 season.
   - The Nets transferred to New Jersey the season after the merger.
   - The Nets were known as the New Jersey Nets after moving to New Jersey.
   - The Nets moved to Brooklyn prior to the 2012-2013 NBA season.
   [Arena Football League]
   - Jim Foster was a promotions manager with the National Football League.
   - Jim Foster conceived of indoor football while watching an indoor soccer match at Madison Square Garden in 1981.
   - Jim Foster wrote his idea on a 9x12 envelope at the game.
   - The envelope included sketches of the field and notes on gameplay.
   - Jim Foster presented his idea to a few friends at the NFL offices.
   - Jim Foster received praise and encouragement for his concept from his friends at the NFL offices.
   - Jim Foster solidified the rules and a business plan for his concept.
   - Jim Foster supplemented his idea with sketches by a professional artist.
   - Jim Foster presented his idea to various television networks.
   - Jim Foster reached an agreement with NBC for a "test game".

Q27: What gun was used by Pollack's director in Westworld?
Gold: LeMat revolver / LeMat Revolver
NARS answer: None
Lost at: no valid rule
Auto-repairs: {'literal_answer': 1}

-- Hop plan (the LLM's, from the question alone)
   1. Who directed Westworld?
   2. What is the full name of #1?
   3. Who is Pollack, and what is their relation to #2?
   4. What gun was used by #2 in Westworld?
-- Answers of the rule sets voted on: [None, None, None]

-- Gold decomposition
   Pollock >> producer -> Ed Harris
   what gun does #1 used in westworld -> LeMat revolver

-- Facts
   [Pollock (film)]
   - Marcia Gay Harden won the Academy Award for Best Supporting Actress for portraying Lee Krasner.
   - Ed Harris received an Academy Award nomination for Best Actor for his portrayal of Pollock.
   - The film was a long-term personal project for Ed Harris.
   - Ed Harris had previously read Pollock's biography.
   - Ed Harris's father noticed that Ed Harris resembled Pollock.
   [LeMat Revolver]
   - The LeMat revolver was a.42 or.36 caliber cap & ball black powder revolver.
   - The LeMat revolver was invented by Jean Alexandre LeMat of New Orleans.
   - The LeMat revolver featured an unusual secondary 20 gauge smooth-bore barrel.
   - The LeMat revolver's secondary barrel was capable of firing buckshot.
   - The LeMat revolver saw service with the armed forces of the Confederate States of America.
   - The LeMat revolver saw service during the American Civil War of 1861-65.
   - The LeMat revolver saw service with the Army of the Government of National Defense.
   - The LeMat revolver saw service during the Franco-Prussian War.

   [Siege of Jerusalem (636–637)]
   - Abu Bakr died in 634.
   - Umar succeeded Abu Bakr in 634.
   - Emperor Heraclius launched a major expedition to regain lost territory in May 636.
   - The Byzantine army was defeated decisively at the Battle of Yarmouk in August 636.
   - Abu Ubaidah held a council of war in early October 636 to discuss future plans.
   - Abu Ubaidah was the Muslim commander-in-chief of the Rashidun army in Syria.
   - The council of war discussed objectives including the coastal city of Caesarea and Jerusalem.
   - Abu Ubaidah wrote to Caliph Umar for instructions on which city to capture.
   - Caliph Umar ordered the capture of Jerusalem.
   - Abu Ubaidah marched towards Jerusalem from Jabiya.
   - Khalid ibn Walid and his mobile guard led the advance towards Jerusalem.
   - The Muslims arrived at Jerusalem around early November 636.
   - The Byzantine garrison withdrew into the fortified city of Jerusalem.

-- Facts
   [The Lord of the Rings (film series)]
   - The Lord of the Rings film series is considered one of the biggest and most ambitious film projects ever undertaken.
   - The overall budget of the Lord of the Rings film series was $281 million.
   - Some sources state that the overall budget of the Lord of the Rings film series was $310 - $330 million.
   - The entire Lord of the Rings film project took eight years to complete.
   - The filming for all three Lord of the Rings films was done simultaneously.
   - The filming for all three Lord of the Rings films was done entirely in New Zealand.
   - New Zealand is Peter Jackson's native country.
   - Each film in the Lord of the Rings series had special extended editions released on DVD.
   - The special extended editions of the Lord of the Rings films were released on DVD a year after their respective theatrical releases.
   - The Lord of the Rings films follow the book's general storyline.
   - The Lord of the Rings films omit some of the novel's plot elements.
   - The Lord of the Rings films include some additions to and deviations from the source material.
   [History of New Zealand]
   - On 6 February 1840, Hobson and about forty Māori chiefs signed the Treaty of Waitangi at Waitangi in the Bay of Islands.
   - Copies of the Treaty of Waitangi were subsequently taken around the country to be signed by other chiefs.
   - A significant number of Māori chiefs refused to sign the Treaty of Waitangi.
   - Some Māori chiefs were not asked to sign the Treaty of Waitangi.
   - More than five hundred Māori eventually signed the Treaty of Waitangi.

-- Facts
   [Empire Sports Network]
   - Empire Sports Network was an American regional sports network.
   - Empire Sports Network was owned by the Adelphia Communications Corporation.
   - Empire Sports Network was available on cable providers in much of upstate New York.
   - Empire Sports Network was available on cable providers in parts of northern Pennsylvania.
   - Empire Sports Network was available on cable providers in parts of eastern Ohio.
   - Empire Sports Network ceased operations on March 7, 2005.
   - Adelphia Communications Corporation was experiencing financial collapse and bankruptcy in 2005.
   [Windjammer Communications]
   - Windjammer Cable is a small cable company.
   - Windjammer Cable was formed by the sale of 25 systems.
   - The 25 systems served 80,000 customers.
   - The 25 systems were in rural areas.
   - Time Warner Cable acquired the 25 systems from Adelphia.
   - Adelphia was bankrupt.
   - Windjammer was created specifically for the deal with Time Warner Cable.
   - MAST Capital Management is a Boston private-equity concern.
   - Communications Construction Services (CCS) is a small cable operator based in Jupiter, Fla.
   - Windjammer consisted of MAST Capital Management and Communications Construction Services (CCS).

Q79: The company making ALARM follows which company?
Gold: BAC
NARS answer: HARM missile
Lost at: rules miss the answer
Auto-repairs: none

-- Hop plan (the LLM's, from the question alone)
   1. What company makes ALARM?
   2. What comes before #1 in the sequence?
-- Answers of the rule sets voted on: ['HARM missile', 'BAC', 'HARM missile']

-- Gold decomposition
   What company makes ALARM? -> British Aerospace
   #1 >> follows -> BAC

-- Facts
   [Sea Wolf (missile)]
   - Sea Wolf is a naval guided missile system.
   - Sea Wolf was designed and built by BAC.
   - BAC later became British Aerospace (BAe) Dynamics.
   - British Aerospace (BAe) Dynamics is now known as MBDA.
   - Sea Wolf is an automated point-defence weapon system.
   - Sea Wolf is designed as a final line of defence against sea-skimming missiles.
   - Sea Wolf is designed as a final line of defence against high angle anti-ship missiles.
   - Sea Wolf is designed as a final line of defence against aircraft.
   - The Royal Navy has fielded the GWS-25 Conventionally Launched Sea Wolf (CLSW) version of Sea Wolf.
   - The Royal Navy has fielded the GWS-26 Vertically Launched Sea Wolf (VLSW) version of Sea Wolf.
   - Sea Wolf is being replaced by Sea Ceptor in Royal Navy service.
   [ALARM]
   - The Ministry of Defence received bids for a new anti-radiation missile in late 1982.
   - British Aerospace Dynamics offered ALARM as a bid for the new anti-radiation missile.
   - Texas Instruments teamed with Lucas Aerospace to offer the HARM missile as a bid for the new anti-radiation missile.
   - Defence Secretary Michael Heseltine announced the selection of ALARM on 29 July 1983.
   - The initial order for ALARM was 750 missiles for the Royal Air Force (RAF).
   - The selection process for the new anti-radiation missile was controversial.
   - The Ministry of Defence favoured ALARM to retain UK industrial capabilities.
   - The Treasury favoured the HARM missile because it was cheaper and proven.

   - The Rebellion of 1088 concerned the division of lands in the Kingdom of England and the Duchy of Normandy.
   - The division of lands was between William the Conqueror's two sons, William Rufus and Robert Curthose.
   - Hostilities of the Rebellion of 1088 lasted from 3 to 6 months.
   - The hostilities of the Rebellion of 1088 started around Easter of 1088.

Q83: Who is the child of Empress Dowager Huang's child?
Gold: Wang Jipeng
NARS answer: Lady Li's mother
Lost at: rules miss the answer
Auto-repairs: none

-- Hop plan (the LLM's, from the question alone)
   1. Who is Empress Dowager Huang's child?
   2. Who is the child of #1?
-- Answers of the rule sets voted on: ["Lady Li's mother", None, None]

-- Gold decomposition
   Empress Dowager Huang >> child -> Wang Yanjun
   #1 >> child -> Wang Jipeng

-- Facts
   [Empress Dowager Huang]
   - Empress Dowager Huang's personal name is unknown.
   - Empress Dowager Huang was referred to semi-formally as Empress Dowager Longqi.
   - Empress Dowager Huang was an empress dowager of the Chinese Five Dynasties and Ten Kingdoms period state Min.
   - Wang Shenzhi was commonly regarded as the founder of the Min state.
   - Wang Shenzhi was also known as Prince Zhongyi.
   - Empress Dowager Huang was the primary concubine of Wang Shenzhi.
   - Wang Yanjun was the son of Empress Dowager Huang.
   - Wang Yanjun was also known as Wang Lin.
   - Wang Yanjun was also known as Emperor Huizong.
   - Wang Yanjun was the first Min ruler to claim the title of emperor.
   [Lady Li (Wang Jipeng)]
   - Lady Li was a cousin to Wang Jipeng.
   - Lady Li's mother was a sister to Wang Yanjun.
   - Wang Yanjun was also known as Wang Lin during his reign as emperor.
   - Lady Li's father was the official Li Min.
   - Li Min served as a chancellor during Wang Lin's reign.
   - Lady Li was referred to as Wang Jipeng's "original princess" (元妃).
   - Wang Jipeng was the Prince of Fu under his father.
   - Lady Li carried the title of Lady of Liang.

-- LLM generations, in order

-- Facts
   [Heartbeat (Nina Sky song)]
   - "Heartbeat" is a song by American twins duo Nina Sky.
   - "Heartbeat" was released as a second single from Nina Sky's third studio album "Nicole and Natalie".
   - "Heartbeat" was released on June 14, 2012.
   - "Heartbeat" was released independently through Nina Sky's official website.
   - The music video for "Heartbeat" was released on July 27, 2012.
   - The album "Nicole and Natalie" was released four days after the music video for "Heartbeat".
   - "Heartbeat: Remixes", a remix EP, was released.
   [Nina Sky]
   - Nina Sky is an American female duo.
   - Nina Sky is composed of identical twins Nicole Albino and Natalie Albino.
   - Nicole Albino and Natalie Albino were born on March 13, 1984.
   - Nicole Albino and Natalie Albino were born in Puerto Rico.
   - Nina Sky's debut single is "Move Ya Body".
   - "Move Ya Body" was released from Nina Sky's self-titled debut album in 2004.
   - "Move Ya Body" reached number 4 on the Billboard Hot 100.
   - Nina Sky's second album is titled "Nicole & Natalie".
   - "Nicole & Natalie" was released on July 31, 2012.
   - Nina Sky's third studio album is titled "Brightest Gold".
   - "Brightest Gold" was announced for release in 2016 through Tommy Boy Entertainment.

-- Facts
   [Malcolm Graham (footballer)]
   - Malcolm Graham joined Barnsley from Hall Green in 1953.
   - Malcolm Graham was working as a miner at Haigh Colliery in 1953.
   - Malcolm Graham stayed with Barnsley for several seasons.
   - Barnsley was relegated at the end of the 1958–59 season.
   - Malcolm Graham left Barnsley after the 1958–59 season.
   - Malcolm Graham had a brief spell at Bristol City.
   - Malcolm Graham moved to Leyton Orient on 20 June 1960.
   - Malcolm Graham was transferred to Leyton Orient for £8000.
   [Mido (footballer)]
   - Mido started his career with Zamalek in Egypt in 1999.
   - Mido left Zamalek for Gent of Belgium in 2000.
   - Mido won the Belgian Ebony Shoe with Gent.
   - Mido moved to Ajax in 2001.
   - Mido joined Celta Vigo on loan in 2003.
   - Mido joined Marseille in France.
   - Mido left Marseille for Italian side Roma in 2004.
   - Mido joined English side Tottenham Hotspur on an 18-month loan in 2005.
   - Mido joined Tottenham Hotspur permanently in 2006.
   - Mido left Tottenham Hotspur in 2007.
   - Mido joined Middlesbrough in 2007.
   - Mido joined Wigan Athletic on loan from Middlesbrough.
   - Mido joined Zamalek on loan from Middlesbrough.
   - Mido joined West Ham United on loan from Middlesbrough.
   - Mido joined Ajax on loan from Middlesbrough.
   - Mido rejoined Zamalek in 2011.
   - Mido joined Barnsley in 2012.
   - Mido played for Egypt 51 times.
   - Mido scored 20 goals for Egypt.
   - Mido retired from football in June 2013.

-- Facts
   [IPLEDGE program]
   - As of March 1, 2006, iPLEDGE patients, their doctors, and their pharmacists are required by the FDA to register and use a web site.
   - The iPLEDGE program requires registration and use of a web site in order to receive, prescribe, or dispense the medication.
   - The iPLEDGE program has reduced exposure of pregnant women to the drug.
   - The iPLEDGE program has been criticized for being overly complicated.
   [FDA Food Safety Modernization Act]
   - The Food Safety Modernization Act (FSMA) was signed into law by President Barack Obama on January 4, 2011.
   - The FSMA has given the Food and Drug Administration (FDA) new authorities to regulate the way foods are grown, harvested and processed.
   - The FSMA grants the FDA mandatory recall authority.
   - The FDA had sought mandatory recall authority for many years.
   - The FSMA requires the FDA to undertake more than a dozen rulemakings.
   - The FSMA requires the FDA to issue at least 10 guidance documents.
   - The FSMA requires the FDA to issue a host of reports, plans, strategies, standards, notices, and other tasks.

   - Consort Dowager Zhao was favored by Liu Yan.

   - Liu Hongdu was Liu Yan's third son.
   - Liu Hongdu's older brothers were Liu Yaoshu and Liu Guitu.
   - Liu Yaoshu and Liu Guitu both died early.
   - During Liu Yan's "Dayou" era, Consort Dowager Zhao received the imperial consort title "Zhaoyi".
   - Liu Yan's "Dayou" era occurred from 928 to 942.
   [Liu Yin (Southern Han)]
   - Liu Yin was born in 874.
   - Liu Yin died on April 4, 911.
   - Liu Yin was formally known as Prince Xiang of Nanhai.
   - Liu Yin was posthumously honored as Emperor Xiang.
   - Liu Yin was given the temple name Liezong by Liu Yan.
   - Liu Yin was a warlord late in the Chinese dynasty Tang Dynasty.
   - Liu Yin was a warlord in the Later Liang dynasty of the Five Dynasties and Ten Kingdoms period.
   - Liu Yin ruled Qinghai Circuit as its military governor.
   - Qinghai Circuit was headquartered in modern Guangzhou, Guangdong.
   - Liu Yin's rule was the basis for Liu Yan's establishment of the state of Southern Han.

   - Vivien Leigh was born Vivian Mary Hartley.
   - Vivien Leigh was also known as Lady Olivier after 1947.
   - Vivien Leigh was born on 5 November 1913.
   - Vivien Leigh died on 8 July 1967.

   - Vivien Leigh won two Academy Awards for Best Actress.
   - Vivien Leigh played the role of Scarlett O'Hara in Gone with the Wind (1939).

-- Facts
   [Charleston, South Carolina]
   - The Jenkins Orphanage Band played in the inaugural parades of Presidents Theodore Roosevelt and William Taft.
   - The Jenkins Orphanage Band toured the USA and Europe.
   - The Jenkins Orphanage Band played on Broadway for the play "Porgy" by DuBose and Dorothy Heyward.
   - The play "Porgy" by DuBose and Dorothy Heyward was a stage version of their novel of the same title.
   - The story of "Porgy" was based in Charleston.
   - The story of "Porgy" featured the Gullah community.
   - DuBose and Dorothy Heyward insisted on hiring the real Jenkins Orphanage Band to portray themselves on stage.
   - DuBose Heyward collaborated with George and Ira Gershwin to turn his novel into the opera Porgy and Bess.
   - George Gershwin and DuBose Heyward spent the summer of 1934 at Folly Beach outside of Charleston writing Porgy and Bess.
   - George Gershwin referred to Porgy and Bess as a "folk opera".
   - Porgy and Bess is considered the Great American Opera.
   - Porgy and Bess is widely performed.
   [Thomas H. Rynning]
   - Thomas Harbo Rynning was born on February 17, 1866.
   - Thomas Harbo Rynning died on June 18, 1941.
   - Thomas Harbo Rynning was an officer in the United States Army.
   - Thomas Harbo Rynning served with Theodore Roosevelt's Rough Riders during the Spanish–American War.
   - Thomas Harbo Rynning was the captain of the Arizona Rangers.
   - Thomas Harbo Rynning was the warden of Yuma Territorial Prison.
   - Thomas Harbo Rynning was a United States Marshal in San Diego, California.

-- Facts
   [South Yemen insurgency]
   - The South Yemen insurgency is a term used by the Yemeni government to describe protests and attacks on government forces in southern Yemen.
   - The South Yemen insurgency has been ongoing since 27 April 2009.
   - 27 April 2009 is South Yemen's independence day.
   - The Yemeni government blames elements within the southern secessionist movement for the violence.
   - Leaders of the southern secessionist movement claim that their aims of independence are to be achieved through peaceful means.
   - Leaders of the southern secessionist movement claim that attacks are from ordinary citizens in response to the government's provocative actions.
   - The South Yemen insurgency is happening at the same time as the Shia insurgency in the country's north.
   - The Shia insurgency in the country's north is led by the Houthi communities.
   - A brief, unsuccessful secession was led by southern leaders in 1994 following unification.
   - Many southern leaders who led the 1994 secession are involved in the present secession movement.
   - Southern separatist insurgents are active mainly in the area of former South Yemen.
   - Southern separatist insurgents are also active in Ad Dali' Governorate.
   - Ad Dali' Governorate was not a part of the independent southern state.
   - The United Arab Emirates supports southern separatist insurgents.
   - The United Arab Emirates is a member of the Saudi Arabian-led coalition working to support the Yemeni government.
   - Abdrabbuh Mansur Hadi is the President of Yemen.
   [People's Vanguard Party (South Yemen)]
   - The People's Vanguard Party was a Ba'athist political party in South Yemen.
   - The People's Vanguard Party was aligned with the Syrian-based Ba'ath Party.
   - Abdallah Badhib was the general secretary of the People's Vanguard Party.
   - Abdallah Badhib was appointed Minister of Education in December 1969.
   - The People's Vanguard Party was one of two non-National Front parties tolerated during the early 1970s.
   - The People's Vanguard Party joined the NF-dominated United Political Organization in October 1975.
   - The United Political Organization evolved into the Yemeni Socialist Party in 1978.
   - The merger of the People's Vanguard Party with the United Political Organization was ratified by the third PVP congress.
   - The third PVP congress was held in August 1975.

-- Gold decomposition
   Who began to increase their coverage of weaker debts? -> The European Central Bank
   Who is the Vice-President of #1 ? -> Vitor Constâncio

-- Facts
   [European Central Bank]
   - The Executive Board is responsible for the implementation of monetary policy defined by the Governing Council.
   - The Executive Board is responsible for the day-to-day running of the European Central Bank.
   - The Executive Board can issue decisions to national central banks.
   - The Executive Board may exercise powers delegated to it by the Governing Council.
   - The Executive Board is composed of the President of the European Central Bank, the Vice-President, and four other members.
   - Mario Draghi is the current President of the European Central Bank.
   - Vitor Constâncio is the current Vice-President of the European Central Bank.
   - Members of the Executive Board are appointed for non-renewable terms of eight years.
   - Members of the Executive Board are appointed from among persons of recognised standing and professional experience in monetary or banking matters.
   - Members of the Executive Board are appointed by common accord of the governments of the Member States at the level of Heads of State or Government.
   - The appointment of members of the Executive Board is made on a recommendation from the Council.
   - The Council consults the European Parliament and the Governing Council of the European Central Bank before recommending the appointment of members of the Executive Board.
   - The Executive Board normally meets every Tuesday.
   [European Central Bank]
   - The European Central Bank had stepped up the buying of member nations' debt.
   - In 2010, a crisis occurred.
   - Proposals have surfaced for a collective European bond issue.
   - A collective European bond issue would allow the European Central Bank to purchase a European version of US Treasury bills.
   - A collective guarantee of the member states' solvency would be necessary to make European sovereign debt assets more similar to a US Treasury.
   - The German government has resisted the proposal for a collective European bond issue.
   - Some analyses indicate that the problems with the euro are due to the linkage between sovereign debt and failing national banking systems.
   - If the European Central Bank were to deal directly with failing banking systems, sovereign debt would not look as leveraged relative to national income in the financially weaker member states.

   - Shen Kuo lived from 1031 to 1095 CE.
   - Shen Kuo formulated a hypothesis for the process of land formation.
   - Shen Kuo observed fossil animal shells in a geological stratum in a mountain hundreds of miles from the ocean.
   - Shen Kuo inferred that land was formed by erosion of mountains and deposition of silt.
   [Muslim world]

   - Islamic philosophy is not necessarily concerned with religious issues.

-- Facts
   [Wedding dress of Princess Elizabeth]
   - The wedding dress of Princess Elizabeth was worn by Princess Elizabeth at her wedding to Philip Mountbatten, Duke of Edinburgh.
   - The wedding of Princess Elizabeth to Philip Mountbatten, Duke of Edinburgh took place on 20 November 1947.
   - The wedding of Princess Elizabeth to Philip Mountbatten, Duke of Edinburgh took place in Westminster Abbey.
   - Clothing was rationed at the time of Princess Elizabeth's wedding.
   - Princess Elizabeth had to purchase the material for her wedding dress using ration coupons.
   - The wedding dress of Princess Elizabeth was designed by Norman Hartnell.
   - Norman Hartnell's signature was embroidery.
   - Norman Hartnell enjoyed working with soft, floating fabrics, particularly tulle and chiffon.
   - Norman Hartnell enjoyed working with plain, lustrous silks.
   - The wedding dress of Princess Elizabeth was made of soft Damascus Prokar.
   - The wedding dress of Princess Elizabeth had a high neckline.
   - The wedding dress of Princess Elizabeth had a tailored bodice.
   - The wedding dress of Princess Elizabeth had a short train.
   [Windsor Castle]
   - Windsor Castle survived the English Civil War.
   - Windsor Castle was used as a military headquarters by Parliamentary forces during the English Civil War.
   - Windsor Castle was used as a prison for Charles I during the English Civil War.
   - Charles II rebuilt much of Windsor Castle with the help of Hugh May at the Restoration of the monarchy in 1660.
   - Hugh May was the architect who helped Charles II rebuild Windsor Castle.
   - Charles II created a set of extravagant Baroque interiors at Windsor Castle.
   - Windsor Castle was neglected during the 18th century.
   - George III and George IV renovated and rebuilt Charles II's palace at Windsor Castle.
   - George III and George IV's renovations of Windsor Castle were done at colossal expense.
   - The current design of the State Apartments at Windsor Castle was produced by George III and George IV's renovations.
   - The State Apartments at Windsor Castle feature Rococo, Gothic, and Baroque furnishings.
   - Queen Victoria made minor changes to Windsor Castle.
   - Windsor Castle was the centre for royal entertainment for much of Queen Victoria's reign.
   - Windsor Castle was used as a refuge by the royal family during the Luftwaffe bombing campaigns of the Second World War.
   - Windsor Castle survived a fire in 1992.
   - Windsor Castle is a popular tourist attraction.
   - Windsor Castle is a venue for hosting state visits.
   - Windsor Castle is the preferred weekend home of Elizabeth II.

-- Facts
   [Ioannina National Airport]
   - Ioannina International Airport is an airport located in Greece.
   - Ioannina International Airport is located four kilometers from the city center of Ioannina.
   - The full name of Ioannina International Airport is Ioannina International Airport - King Pyrrhus.
   [Hieronymus of Cardia]
   - Hieronymus of Cardia wrote a history of the Diadochi and their descendants.
   - The history written by Hieronymus of Cardia encompassed the period from the death of Alexander to the war with Pyrrhus.
   - The period covered by Hieronymus of Cardia's history was from 323 to 272 BC.
   - Diodorus Siculus used Hieronymus of Cardia's history as one of his chief authorities in books xviii.–xx.
   - Plutarch used Hieronymus of Cardia's history in his life of Pyrrhus.

-- Facts
   [Queen (band)]
   - Brian May announced in a BBC interview in September 2010 that Sacha Baron Cohen was to play Mercury in a film.
   - Sacha Baron Cohen was chosen to play Mercury in a film.
   - Time commented with approval on Sacha Baron Cohen's singing ability and visual similarity to Mercury.
   - Sacha Baron Cohen dropped out of the role of Mercury in July 2013 due to "creative differences".
   - The "creative differences" were between Sacha Baron Cohen and the surviving band members.
   - Ben Whishaw was chosen to replace Sacha Baron Cohen in the role of Mercury in December 2013.
   - Ben Whishaw is best known for playing Q in the James Bond film Skyfall.
   - Peter Morgan is writing the screenplay for the film about Queen.
   - Peter Morgan was nominated for Oscars for his screenplays The Queen and Frost/Nixon.
   - The film about Queen is being co-produced by Robert De Niro's TriBeCa Productions.
   - The film about Queen will focus on Queen's formative years and the period leading up to the 1985 Live Aid concert.
   [The Godfather Part II]
   - The Godfather Part II is a 1974 American crime film.
   - Francis Ford Coppola produced and directed The Godfather Part II.
   - Francis Ford Coppola co-wrote the screenplay of The Godfather Part II with Mario Puzo.
   - The Godfather Part II stars Al Pacino and Robert De Niro.
   - The Godfather Part II is partially based on Mario Puzo's 1969 novel The Godfather.
   - The Godfather Part II is both a sequel and a prequel to The Godfather.
   - The Godfather Part II presents parallel dramas.
   - One of the dramas in The Godfather Part II picks up the 1958 story of Michael Corleone.
   - Michael Corleone is the new Don of the Corleone crime family in The Godfather Part II.
   - Michael Corleone protects the family business in The Godfather Part II after an attempt on his life.
   - The prequel in The Godfather Part II covers the journey of Vito Corleone from his Sicilian childhood.
   - Vito Corleone's journey in The Godfather Part II ends with the founding of his family enterprise in New York City.

NARS answer: Tube Alloys project
Lost at: rules miss the answer
Auto-repairs: none

-- Answers of the rule sets voted on: ['Tube Alloys project', 'Tube Alloys project']

-- Gold decomposition
   who discovered that all atoms of an element have the same number of protons -> James Chadwick
   #1 >> participant in -> Manhattan Project

-- Facts
   [Atomic number]
   - James Chadwick discovered the neutron in 1932.
   - An atom of gold contains 118 neutrons.
   - An atom of gold contains 79 protons.
   - An element's atomic number Z is identical to the proton number of its nuclei.
   [James Chadwick]
   - James Chadwick carried out research as part of the Tube Alloys project during the Second World War.
   - James Chadwick's Manchester lab and environs were harassed by Luftwaffe bombing during the Second World War.
   - The Quebec Agreement merged the Tube Alloys project with the American Manhattan Project.
   - James Chadwick became part of the British Mission after the Quebec Agreement.
   - James Chadwick worked at the Los Alamos Laboratory.
   - James Chadwick worked in Washington, D.C.
   - James Chadwick earned the almost-complete trust of Leslie R. Groves, Jr.
   - James Chadwick received a knighthood in the New Year Honours on 1 January 1945.
   - James Chadwick viewed the Trinity nuclear test in July 1945.
   - James Chadwick served as the British scientific advisor to the United Nations Atomic Energy Commission.
   - James Chadwick became the Master of Gonville and Caius College in 1948.
   - James Chadwick retired in 1959.

   - Miki Asakura recorded the song "ヒーロー HOLDING OUT FOR A HERO" in Japanese in 1984.
   - Elizabeth Daily recorded the song as the series theme song to the TV series Cover Up.
   - Jennifer Saunders recorded the song for the 2004 film Shrek 2.
   - The song was featured on the soundtrack to the 2004 film Shrek 2.
   - Frou Frou recorded an alternatively tuned version of the song for the 2004 film Shrek 2.
   - Frou Frou's version of the song appears during the credits of the 2004 film Shrek 2.
   - Frou Frou's version of the song appears on the soundtrack to the 2004 film Shrek 2.
   - Emery recorded a cover of the song for the 2005 Fearless Records compilation Punk Goes 80's.
   - Ella Mae Bowen recorded a country version of the song.
   - Ella Mae Bowen's version of the song appeared on the soundtrack to the 2011 remake of Footloose.
